# Solidified Context — Fact Ledger

Tier-0 spine. Read this with `docs/loop-state.md` before acting. This is the one authoritative
ledger of what is **Verified**, what is an **Assumption**, and what is **Open**. It references the
other docs as evidence rather than restating them. `npm run verify:context-freshness` gates this
file: every `Verified` row needs evidence and an ISO date, evidence paths must exist, supersedes
must resolve, no superseded rule may still read as active, and `docs/loop-state.md` must stay a
short, current pointer.

Conventions: `F-` verified fact · `A-` assumption · `Q-` open question. `status` is one of
`Verified` / `Assumption` / `Open`. Dates are ISO (`YYYY-MM-DD`). `supersedes` points at the row id
this replaced, or `—`. `review-by` is the date after which a `Verified` row must be re-checked (a
non-failing staleness warning), or `—`.

| F-BUDGET-1          | SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29 by `F-COST-CEILING-S52`; retained as history. It read: the cloud budget is a hard flat total cap enforced by a real kill switch (budget → Pub/Sub → a Cloud Function that disables billing), not alerts alone. The kill-switch CHAIN remains true and armed — verified end to end on 2026-07-29. What was wrong: the cap was per calendar month, not a lifetime total; the hard stop is the smaller of the budget amount and the guardrail's own `KILL_SWITCH_CAP_USD`, so the budget alone never set it; `scripts/check-budget-guard.mjs` enforces posture, not any dollar amount; and disabling billing on the production project is a total outage that notifies nobody. | F-DEMO-FIRESTORE-GUARD | Demo Firestore seed/reset/operator paths fail closed through one pre-Admin target guard. The effective `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` may come from the process or `.env.local`, but it must parse as loopback, be reachable, and is then normalized into the process environment before any dynamic Firebase Admin import/initialization or child process. A project id is an emulator namespace only and never grants a live fallback; demo commands have no `--live` mode. | F-MAINT-INTAKE-PUBLIC | The public tokenized maintenance intake (A5) is the app's ONE unauthenticated write endpoint, and it cannot reach the edit gate or a system of record. A staff member mints an HMAC-signed token (edit-gated `POST /api/maintenance/intake/token` or `npm run intake:mint`) scoped to a property; `POST /api/maintenance/intake/public` accepts it in an `X-Intake-Token` header and, only if valid, writes to the `maintenance_unverified_intake` QUARANTINE collection through the no-actor `createUnverifiedIntakeFromPublic` writer — which takes NO actor and imports neither `createMaintenanceTicket`, `requireCapability`, `lib/auth/session`, RentVine, nor the unit-matcher (pinned by a negative-import test), so an external reporter can never create a real ticket or trigger a send/SoR write. Token verify uses a length-guarded `timingSafeEqual` (a forged token yields a generic 401, never a 500/timing oracle), a version-domain-separated HMAC over the literal payload bytes, and a verify-side 30-day max-TTL. Abuse controls are transactional (reads-before-writes): a single-use nonce (jti) blocks replay, a per-property daily cap is the 503 kill-ceiling (owner budget safety), and a revocation epoch invalidates outstanding tokens; an in-instance token-bucket pre-gate keyed on the salted rightmost-XFF IP sheds bursts before any HMAC. Bodies stream under a hard 16 KB cap; every field is NFC-normalized + stripped of controls/bidi/zero-width + CSV-injection-neutralized; the 202 returns a fresh random reference (never the doc id or jti). Both mint and public submit fail CLOSED with generic 503 until `MAINTENANCE_INTAKE_TOKEN_SECRET` and `MAINTENANCE_INTAKE_IP_HASH_SALT` resolve to distinct values of at least 32 UTF-8 bytes; the token primitive itself refuses a weak signing secret. The route ships `production_allowed:false` (no registry action — it is an app-plane quarantine write, not an external action). A new enumerating `tests/unit/route-auth-boundary.test.ts` asserts every `app/api/**/route.ts` is authed except an explicit 3-entry allow-list (`auth/session`, `auth/demo`, this route). Verified locally: full unit suite + the emulator rules test (all client writes to intake/activity denied; nonce/counter/epoch server-only). | Verified | `lib/maintenance/intake-token.ts`; `lib/maintenance/intake-sanitize.ts`; `lib/maintenance/intake-client-ip.ts`; `lib/maintenance/intake-rate-limit.ts`; `lib/firestore/maintenance-unverified-intake.ts`; `app/api/maintenance/intake/public/route.ts`; `app/api/maintenance/intake/token/route.ts`; `scripts/mint-maintenance-intake-token.ts`; `firestore.rules`; `tests/unit/route-auth-boundary.test.ts`; `tests/firestore/maintenance-intake.rules.test.ts` | 2026-07-30 | — | 2026-12-31 |

| F-NOTIF-FRAMEWORK | An in-app notification framework unifies the app's notifications (console overhaul Slice 3b): a client-safe family catalog (`NOTIFICATION_FAMILIES`) with two AVAILABLE in-app families (Approvals, Maintenance tickets) and two STUBBED families (RentVine replies, Owner replies) rendered available:false with "Waiting on Gmail access". A pure `buildNotificationFeed` merges the per-user approval-queue + maintenance notifications newest-first, drops muted families, and returns the family views; read-gated `/api/notifications` serves it, `/api/notifications/mark-read` dispatches the per-source read flip. B4 — an additive low-alarm layer on `user_notification_preferences` (`lane_thresholds`/`snoozed_lanes`/`digest_lanes`, MERGE-PRESERVED so a mute-only bell PATCH never wipes them) plus a pure resolver (per-lane min-severity threshold, snooze-until, digest collapse of N rows to one). B6 — `POST /api/notifications/mark-all-read` (read-gated, self + space-scoped) that flips every unread EVENT notification and leaves the standing families untouched, plus richer per-lane all-clear copy. B7 — the S16-scope-filtered Console deck is lane-stamped (every card + row carries a `data-lane` from the closed enum); this suite adds NO scope filter of its own (S16 owns it, AC-S16-4) and NO new approve/send/write affordance beyond the existing A4 in-place approve. | F-COMMUNICATIONS-POLICY-BUILT | S24 is Local green. `communications-retention:v1.0` separates 10-minute confirmation usability from 30-day record deletion and encodes 7-day dedupe, 90-day sync, 365-day refreshed links, and 7-year bodyless audit. Every newly written or successfully migrated canonical row carries Firestore Date/Timestamp `expires_at` plus matching numeric `expires_at_ms`; legacy rows remain gated on the migration, and legal hold nulls both while release restores both from policy. Cleanup uses bounded indexed numeric-expiry queries, rechecks each candidate transactionally, and atomically records bodyless processed hashes/counts with deletion in a resumable run ledger before finalizing a counts-only audit. Each run freezes its original ordered candidate hashes and limit; an audit-outage retry can process only that set, a hash crowded out of the bounded resume query is explicitly failed/accounted for a later new run, completed reuse makes no mutation, and strict processed/failed/deleted bounds prevent over-deletion or stale immutable audit counts. The cleanup-run ledger itself is an eight-target, seven-year bodyless-audit retention/TTL target. A bodyless legacy-TTL migration projection is built. The runnable worker requires an explicit emulator acknowledgement, non-production mode, and a loopback Firestore emulator. Exactly three frozen v1.0 artifact hashes render through approved generators and block non-authoritative recipient/mailbox/source, missing values, `Needs Verification`, and unreceipted channel-success claims. `workflow-reply:v1.0` is explicit-request, excluded-category-safe, transient, source-visible, diffed, and rejects unsupported claims. A reasoned exact preview binds the current Firebase UID, lifecycle status, and `inviteVersion`. A transactional, per-request claimed/prepared/completed marker with a two-minute lease ensures only one active request can mutate Firebase or mint a setup link; overlap and completed replay fail before auth mutation, and an expired claim is recoverable only after the deployed 60-second request lifetime. The winning initial claim atomically appends bodyless `test_vendor_authentication_reset_claimed`; a failed post-claim/pre-commit attempt truthfully retains only that claim event. Both audits contain actor UID/Vendor id/reason hash/time and never a target/replacement Firebase UID, link, secret, or plaintext reason. Compensation re-hardens only while the request still owns the lease, so a loser cannot disable a winner. The stable Vendor id, Test tickets, assignments, mailbox history, and completed receipts remain; the old password, TOTP factors, sessions, action links, and UID-bound confirmations become invalid. A prepared repair changes only the UID, preserving its already committed single invite increment and canonical `test_vendor_authentication_reset` audit; the separate recovery-claim audit is not a duplicate reset audit. | F-S26-DRAFT-CONTRACT-ALIGNED | The maintenance owner-notice draft is now resolvable and correctly contracted, which is the prerequisite for migrating the draft pair onto the S20 execution contract. `gmail.maintenance_owner_notice.draft_create` existed in the Action Registry, risk policy, and executor but was absent from BOTH canonical execution matrices, so the S20 bridge's `resolveCanonicalExternalDefinition` would have rejected it as `action_unknown` and the action could never have run through the canonical contract. It is appended (never inserted — the maintenance definitions bind by array index, so a mid-array insert would silently rebind every later definition) with group Owner email, Medium risk matching `EXECUTION_ACTION_POLICIES`, the correction "Delete the unsent draft.", and NO dependency: the paired send depends on `rentvine.work_order.create`, which is Needs Connection, so inheriting that dependency would have made the draft permanently unsatisfiable in the same way the label action was. Delivery is now three separate, individually reviewable invocations. `--plan-only` prints the ordered plan and is a GUARANTEED non-executing branch: argument errors are decided before any environment work, and a test replaces `node:child_process.spawn` with a throwing spy to prove no path reaches a process. `--execute` captures the currently serving revision as the rollback target FIRST, then deploys the candidate with `--no-traffic` and its own `--tag`, so the revision exists and is reachable at its tag URL while zero user traffic reaches it — and then deliberately STOPS. `--promote` is a separate invocation that targets the exact revision via `--to-revisions=<name>=100`, never `--to-latest`, and prints the exact rollback command restoring the captured prior revision. Combining `--plan-only` with an executing flag, or `--execute` with `--promote`, is a hard refusal rather than a precedence rule. Both target environments carry their explicit `ENVIRONMENT_KIND`/`DATA_CONTEXT` pair so a deployed revision resolves `source:"explicit"` and never `legacy-node-env`, and a resolved value contradicting the named environment is refused. | F-COST-CEILING-S52-APPLIED | S52's ceiling is SET and live as of 2026-08-01, superseding the null-value state in `F-COST-CEILING-S52`. Owner-approved values: alert `$25`, hard stop `$100`. The baseline is measured, not invented: July 2026 was the first complete calendar month with the app deployed throughout (the earliest date the policy allowed), and the `budget-guardrail` function's own logs — which record the real `costAmount` from every Cloud Billing notification, roughly every 25 minutes — read `costAmount 0 USD` on every notification across 30 days, including July's last at `2026-07-31T23:54Z`. The decoder was verified rather than trusted: a missing field logs the distinct string "no numeric costAmount", so the zero is a parsed value, not a silent default. Both enforcement points were moved and read back, because `infra/budget-guardrail/handler.mjs` applies the SMALLER of the two: budget `pmi-kc-kb-prod hard stop 100USD` at `$100` and `KILL_SWITCH_CAP_USD=100` on the guardrail, giving an effective stop of `min(100, 100)`. Either move order is safe, since the ceiling only rises once both have risen. A `$25` alert-only budget and a `$100` account-wide backstop both notify `josiah@pmikcmetro.com` and `dan@pmikcmetro.com` through Cloud Monitoring channels; the account-wide one exists because the kill switch is project-scoped and cannot see spend in `adept-primacy-499822-d7`. `$100` rather than something tighter because the stop's only behavior is disabling billing — a total outage, not auto-reversible, notifying nobody — so a stop far above measured burn is the safe error; the per-user throttles do not bound monthly spend (`/api/ask` refills at 0.5 token/s, roughly 1,800 calls/hour/user sustained). OPEN CAVEAT: all three budgets use `INCLUDE_ALL_CREDITS` and both projects date to 2026-06-18/19, so a 90-day trial credit could be masking gross spend; no BigQuery billing export exists in either project and such exports never backfill, so July's gross figure exists only in the Console report. Re-review at the first month reporting a non-zero `costAmount`. KNOWN EXPIRY: the guardrail runs Node.js 20, decommissioned on 30 October 2026; if it stops running the kill switch is inert while every budget still reads as configured. GCLOUD TRAP, hit and caught by readback on 2026-08-01: `thresholdRules[].thresholdPercent` is 1.0-based (1.0 = 100%), but `gcloud billing budgets update --add-threshold-rule=percent=N` documents `percent` as an integer 0-100 and passes it through unchanged, so `percent=100` stores `100.0` = 10,000% and would not have fired until `$2,500` on a `$25` budget while reading as configured. ## Open Questions

The `Q-` rows above, surfaced together so a reader sees the unknowns at a glance. Detail and history
live in `docs/research-backlog.md` and `docs/status.md`; resolve a question by flipping its row to a
`Verified` fact (with evidence + date) or recording why it stays open.

- **Q-ABC-1** — RETIRED 2026-07-29 (D48): never defined, nothing depends on it, closed rather than carried.
- **Q-KBCORR-2** — RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (D46): no default review interval; honest "reviewed &lt;date&gt;" only.
- **Q-SUPP-FOLLOWUP** — RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (D47): 1-day / 3-day calendar thresholds accepted as shipped.
- **Q-RENTCAST-ENDPOINT** — PARTIALLY RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (D40): owner takes the free tier and stores the key; free-tier rate limits, search radius, and minimum comp count remain open.
- **Digest completeness caveat.** This digest historically lagged the ledger — eight `Q-` ids added since 2026-07-23 were absent from it, so a reader trusting the digest alone would have concluded only `Q-ABC-1` was open. Treat the ledger rows above as authoritative and this list as a convenience index; S54 owns making the two consistent.
- **Q-V1-EDITOR-EXECUTION / Q-V1-ENDTOEND-BOUNDARY** — RESOLVED by the working-app model. S20/S25/S26 define the exact Live contracts; persistent production Test workflows prove app execution, while each real provider action activates independently.
- **Q-SOURCE-PUBLISH-BOUNDARY** — RESOLVED by R05; S21 defines the automated trust boundary.
- **Q-VENDOR-PORTAL-IDENTITY** — RESOLVED by R04/S22: Admin-provisioned Vendor identity, verified email, TOTP, assigned-ticket authorization, and isolated Test mailbox are application V1; each real Vendor's OAuth mailbox is activated independently.
- **Q-CONSOLE-REAL-DATA** — RESOLVED by R08/S23: production renders bounded Live data and clearly labeled, isolated, persisted Test workflow state together.
- **Q-GMAIL-RETENTION / Q-GMAIL-RENEWAL-ARTIFACTS / Q-GMAIL-MAINT-ARTIFACTS** — RESOLVED by R06/R07/S24: exact bodyless retention/legal hold, the three v1.0 base artifacts, and human-confirmed exact reply policy are implemented; automatic TTL/Scheduler operation is optional.
- **Q-PREC-1** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-01): per-case manual override via the resolve flow, Admin-approved for High-severity, reason mandatory + logged, never auto-applied.
- **Q-WRITEBACK-METHOD** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-01): append-only proposal column first; graduate to cell-anchored compare-and-set after Phase-1 accuracy is proven; RentVine-first deferred (OQ-RV-1).
- **Q-MAINT-STORAGE** — RESOLVED: Drive sync live + verified (F-DRIVE-DWD); only the prod Cloud Run MAINTENANCE_PHOTO_DRIVE_FOLDER_ID env remains (deploy step), now enforced by the cutover preflight.
- **Q-MAINT-PHOTO-INDEX** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-01): binding rule — tenant photos never enter an indexed corpus; SOPs index from a separate approved low-sensitivity source only.
- **D1-APPROVAL-REASON** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-10): in the S14 mobile approval redesign, a tapped `reason_code` satisfies the mandatory-reason rule for Low/Medium severity (stamped verbatim to the audit record); free-text reason stays REQUIRED for High/Blocked and any manual override. Scopes but does not remove Q-PREC-1.
- **D2-ATTENTION-IA** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-10): Console stays the at-a-glance home; Notifications becomes a superset time-ordered hub the same signals ALSO flow into (ADD, not MOVE), backed by one shared gather so counts never diverge (S17).
- **D3-GMAIL-HUB** — SUPERSEDED 2026-07-13 by F-GMAIL-LIVE-DIRECTION. S15's pasted/sanitized app-plane remains a fallback; the final-state direction is bounded per-user live Gmail with exact-confirmation send.
- **D4-OVERHAUL-CADENCE** — RESOLVED (owner 2026-07-10): the 2026-07-10 cycle delivers the runner-agnostic governance foundation + specs S14–S18 only, then STOPS for owner review before any pillar implementation.
- **Q-RBAC-SCOPE-MODEL** — RESOLVED for managed staff by Round 1 safe-default approval: keep the orthogonal `scopes` claim and absent=all-spaces behavior. The new vendor-worker identity/mailbox is separate (`Q-VENDOR-PORTAL-IDENTITY`).
- **Q-RBAC-1** — RESOLVED by Round 1 safe-default approval: scope-denied users land on their primary in-scope space.
- **Q-DAN-REVIEW-CADENCE** — RESOLVED by Round 1 safe-default approval: weekly, renewals-only for V1.
